### Round 22 - 2026-10-07 - Phase 3 (Tasks 3.1–3.4), `/review-loop` round 3 of cap 3 — re-review attacking rounds 20–21's classes (stage-3 executor)

- Round status: Closed by the stage-3 executor 2026-10-07 (6 applied, 3 no change; 0 pending); CONVERGED by the loop's rule (no CRIT/HIGH/MED) on the cap round — the fixes' verification is the FULL desktop suite, composer-run, requested 2026-10-07
- Source: Claude Code `/review-loop` (`claude-opus-5-5`, `/execute-loop` run stage-3 executor; 3 foreground lens subagents — custody and failure paths (classes A–D), text, docs, pins and ambient state (E–G), regression of round 21's fixes; every candidate re-read against the code before it was logged)
- Scope: the whole uncommitted Phase 3 working tree over `756b17f`, after round 21's fixes
- Review baseline: `756b17f` + the uncommitted Phase 3 working tree (round 21 selection 1159 passed / 9 skipped, composer-run)
- Round classification: 🆕 new sites of the rounds 20–21 classes (failure path before the clean-up; a line that blames the wrong cause; pins another spelling slips past; ambient-state fixes with no pin), plus two stale docstrings from round 21 (fix-induced). Dropped at verification: `realpath` judged vs written folder (both `os.path.realpath`; the retarget during the shadow question is the same-user residue); Delete now ignoring its kept write (C2, Phase 2); `st_ino` 0 on FAT; `EXPORT_APP_FOLDER` plus "Choose a folder on this computer's own drive" (not untrue); `PAST_SESSION_REASONS` / design-system strings otherwise word for word; `getSaveFileName(options=)` valid and never reached by tests; every `ExportRecovery` consumer default-safe.

#### Findings

- **LOW-001** (`past_sessions.py` `_remove_part`): an external drive reported as fixed (MED-003's residue) unplugged at the next start reads "not found", so the row is dropped and a `.part` on that drive is forgotten. /fix decision: No change — keeping the row while the drive is absent would refuse every later export for ever if the drive never returns (the trade round 20 LOW-005 already weighed); named for Task 5.3a with MED-003's drive residues.
- **LOW-002** (`past_sessions.py` `_export`): the deferred `speech` import and `os.fdopen` sat after the exclusive create and before the clean-up handler — a failure there leaked the descriptor, escaped raw and left an empty `<id>.wav.part` that `export_part_exists` would then refuse for ever. /fix decision: Applied — the import moved before the create; a failing `fdopen` closes the descriptor, removes the empty file by name and raises `export_failed` (`export_cleanup_failed` if it stays); `test_an_export_file_that_cannot_be_opened_as_a_stream_is_removed`.
- **LOW-003** (`ui/past_sessions.py` `_delete_recording`): a deliberate Delete recording records kept and deleted but never `archived`, so a Complete whose own audit write failed loses that fact once the recording's files are gone. /fix decision: No change — Delete recording writes its own facts only (the Phase 2 decision pinned by `test_delete_recording_takes_two_clicks_and_keeps_the_transcript_and_notes`); a fact lost to an audit outage is the privacy plan's C2 residue, as for every attended writer.
- **LOW-004** (`past_sessions.py` `_drop_export_row` / `ui/past_sessions.py`): a kill between the rename and the UI's `record_recording_exported` leaves an exported WAV with no count, and the row is already gone. /fix decision: No change — the export count is a content-free tally written by the deliberate writer after the file is in place (Task 3.3); an audit lost to a kill or an outage is C2's residue; named for Task 5.3a.
- **LOW-005** (`tests/test_past_sessions.py`): round 21's folder resolve had no test (`tmp_path` is already canonical), and `test_a_relative_folder_is_refused` could not fail (a missing `cwd\out` made the create fail with the same reason). /fix decision: Applied — `test_the_resolved_folder_is_exported_into_and_recorded` (through a junction: the row and the file name the target), `test_a_folder_that_cannot_be_resolved_writes_nothing`, and the relative test now `chdir`s into a folder that EXISTS and asserts it stays empty.
- **LOW-006** (`past_sessions.py` `PAST_SESSION_REASONS["export_ledger_busy"]`): "if this keeps happening, restart the computer" promised a fix a restart cannot give for the persistent causes (a deny ACL, a folder in its place). /fix decision: Applied — "the record of earlier exports could not be opened - try again in a moment" (the start-up busy line already says it checks again); design-system quote updated. (A non-file at `exports.enc` stays busy, never reset: same-user tampering, the standing residue.)
- **LOW-007** (`past_sessions.py` `_recording_chunks`): a recording failing part-way through (a damaged later chunk) read "that Past-sessions entry cannot be read on this Windows account" — blaming the account, though the entry key unwrapped. /fix decision: Applied — a failure after audio has been read is `recording_unreadable` ("the kept recording could not be read to the end, so it may be damaged - the transcript and notes are unaffected, and Delete recording removes it"); before any audio it stays `unreadable`; `test_a_real_corrupt_chunk_on_disk_leaves_no_file` pins it; design-system bullet extended.
- **LOW-008** (`tests/test_shadow_exits.py`): pins other spellings slip past — `from scribe_desktop.speech import wave as w`; references (not calls) to `read_recording`; the private `_recording_chunks` / `_audio_chunks`; `validation_set`'s public wrappers of the writer. /fix decision: Applied for the re-exported `wave` (`_wave_imports` flags any `from <module> import wave`; a fourth negative case). No change for the rest: the private helpers are reached only through `read_recording` and the staging verification, and `validation_set`'s wrappers take invented PCM — the reader pin is the gate on a kept recording's plaintext; deliberate evasion is review's (round 21's call).
- **LOW-009** (`past_sessions.py` `recover_exports`, `ui/past_sessions_view.py` `export_recovery_lines`): docstrings did not name round 21's busy outcome. /fix decision: Applied.

- Slice C (the Past sessions tab: marker, review reminder, Delete recording, Export's dialog, shadow confirm, messages): `ui/{past_sessions,past_sessions_view,main_window}.py`, `docs/design-system.md`; test `test_ui_screens.py` — 1 claim checked, 1 confirmed, 0 dropped
- Peer-ID map (journal grammar): A01 → PR-HIGH-001, A02 → PR-LOW-002, B01 → PR-MED-003, B02 → PR-MED-004, B03 → PR-MED-005, C01 → PR-LOW-006. PR-HIGH-001 is a check-then-use gap on the export destination (the folder is checked, then re-resolved by the store after the shadow confirm); the three MEDs are planted-site escapes from the exit pins (aliases, stored references, dynamic imports) — the peer states no current leak is demonstrated.

#### Findings

- **PR-HIGH-001** (slice A01) (HIGH, behavioral, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/past_sessions.py:1176`): Export re-resolves the destination after its custody check, allowing a changed junction or drive mapping to redirect plaintext into a forbidden folder. The shadow-confirmation dialog leaves this interval open while awaiting input. — Evidence: `exclusions.py:489` resolves the checked folder but returns no canonical destination; the caller at `ui/past_sessions.py:776–785` calls `check_export_location(layer, folder)`, then `self._confirm(...)`, then `store.export_recording(session_id, folder)`. The store subsequently executes `folder = Path(os.path.realpath(folder))` without checking this newly resolved destination. Recommendation: Fix-now — Bind the export to the checked canonical destination and validate it after confirmation before creating the file; cover a mapping changed during confirmation. Executor verification: surface=production, rec=Fix-now — confirmed: `on_export_recording` ran `check_export_location(layer, folder)`, then the shadow / unknown-label `_confirm` (a modal wait on the user), then `store.export_recording(session_id, folder)`, and `_export` re-resolved `os.path.realpath(folder)` with no check — a junction retargeted or a `subst` / mapped drive changed during the question put the plaintext WAV where the check would refuse; the HIGH grade holds (a plaintext consultation off custody, though it needs a same-user change inside the wait). /fix decision: Applied (high-auto=on: a verified Fix-now). /fix notes: `export_recording(session_id, folder, *, check_destination)` — the check is a REQUIRED keyword (every caller must give one; none means no export), run by the store on the RESOLVED folder as the last step before the exclusive create (after the ledger resolution and the `export_exists` check); a refusal line, or a check that raises, refuses with the new `ExportDestinationRefused(PastSessionError)` (`export_destination_refused`, carrying the check's own line) — nothing created, no row; the tab passes `_destination_check(layer)` (the same `check_export_location`) and keeps its own check before the question (refused before asking); the view shows the store's refusal exactly as the tab's would (`export_refused_line(line)`, "could not check" when the check could not be made). Residue: the instant between the store's check and the create (same-user), named for Task 5.3a. Tests: `test_the_resolved_folder_is_checked_just_before_the_create` (a junction retargeted from an allowed to a refused folder after the caller's check: refused, the RESOLVED folder is what was checked, both folders empty, no row), `test_a_destination_check_that_raises_refuses`, the view mapping in `test_each_refusal_names_the_file_it_is_about`, and the tab's `test_a_folder_changed_during_the_question_is_refused_by_the_store` (the layer's mapping moves into OneDrive inside the confirm: the OneDrive line, nothing written, nothing counted); every store-level export test now passes the check explicitly. /fix date: 2026-10-07. /fix applied by: Claude Code (stage-3 executor).
- **PR-LOW-002** (slice A02) (LOW, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_past_sessions.py:3203`): The recovery test removes its leftover file during setup, so its claimed recovery-deletion check passes vacuously. — Evidence: `left = _left_part(store, tmp_path, sid)` is followed by `store.export_recording(done, tmp_path)` before `store.recover_exports()`. Export already calls `remaining = self._resolve(rows)` (`past_sessions.py:1185`), removing that partial file and clearing its row. Recommendation: Fix-now — Create the finished WAV before creating the leftover partial file, and assert the partial file and ledger row exist immediately before recovery. Executor verification: surface=test-harness, rec=Fix-now — confirmed: `_export` resolves earlier rows first, so the setup's export removed the leftover and the recovery assertions held vacuously. /fix decision: Applied. /fix notes: the finished export is made first, the leftover after it, and the test asserts the `.part` and its row exist immediately before `recover_exports`. /fix date: 2026-10-07. /fix applied by: Claude Code (stage-3 executor).
- **PR-MED-003** (slice B01) (MED, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_shadow_exits.py:789`): The writer-caller pin counts imports rather than calls through imported aliases. Additional calls to the existing `speech_write_wav` alias, or another module importing that alias from `validation_set`, leave the count unchanged. The alias is reachable today (`validation_set.py:60`); no unchecked export is demonstrated. — Evidence: `"if any(alias.name in (\"write_wav\", \"*\") for alias in node.names):"` and `"elif isinstance(node, ast.Attribute) and node.attr == \"write_wav\":"`; the actual caller is `"speech_write_wav(buffer, (pcm,))"` (`validation_set.py:315`). Recommendation: Fix-now — Resolve imported aliases and re-exports to count actual writer calls, with planted additional-call checks. Executor verification: surface=test-harness, rec=Fix-now — confirmed: `_write_wav_users` counted imports and `.write_wav` attributes only; a second `speech_write_wav(...)` call, or `from scribe_desktop.validation_set import speech_write_wav`, left it unchanged. /fix decision: Applied. /fix notes: ONE structural rule replaces the per-shape matchers — `_references(sources, names)` counts, per scope, EVERY reference to a guarded name: by its own name, by any alias an import gives it anywhere in the package, as an attribute, as an import of that name from ANY module (a re-export), and as a string equal to it (`getattr`); called, stored or passed alike. The writer's names are `write_wav` and `validation_set`'s public `wav_bytes` (both reach the one writer); `_WRITER_REFERENCES` pins today's eight references in five scopes (the import and call in `PastSessionStore._export`; `validation_set`'s aliased import, two `__all__` strings and the three synthetic-PCM uses). Negative cases: an extra call through the existing alias, a re-export of the alias, a stored reference, `getattr(speech, 'write_wav')`, an import of `wav_bytes`, and round 21's three. The docstring names the residue an AST count cannot see (a reference built at run time — a computed string, `exec` / `eval`, an object handed in from outside), recorded for Task 5.3a. /fix date: 2026-10-07. /fix applied by: Claude Code (stage-3 executor).
- **PR-MED-004** (slice B02) (MED, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_shadow_exits.py:775`): The recording-reader pin misses a bound method saved under another name and subsequently called. Acquiring the method is an attribute reference, while invoking its alias no longer has the callee name `read_recording`; both escape this condition. This is a planted-site escape using the existing API, not a demonstrated current plaintext leak. — Evidence: `"if isinstance(node, ast.Call) and _callee(node) == \"read_recording\":"`; `_callee` returns `"func.id"` for a name (`test_shadow_exits.py:80`). Recommendation: Fix-now — Pin acquisition of recording-reader references or resolve their aliases, and add a stored-method mutation case. Executor verification: surface=test-harness, rec=Fix-now — confirmed: `r = store.read_recording` is an attribute reference, not a call named `read_recording`, so a stored bound method escaped. /fix decision: Applied. /fix notes: the same `_references` rule over the kept recording's three plaintext readers (`read_recording` and the store's private `_recording_chunks` / `_audio_chunks`), pinned at their four references today (`export_recording` → `read_recording` → `_recording_chunks` → `_audio_chunks`, plus the staging verification's digest in `_verify_staged`); negative cases: a stored bound method, `getattr(store, 'read_recording')`, a direct `_recording_chunks` call, a second read in `export_recording`, and round 21's two. /fix date: 2026-10-07. /fix applied by: Claude Code (stage-3 executor).
- **PR-MED-005** (slice B03) (MED, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_shadow_exits.py:665`): The WAV-site and import pins jointly miss saving the existing `wave.open` reference and calling it through a local name. No new import is necessary because `speech.py:33` already imports `wave`. Dynamic imports also escape the import matcher. These are undetected planted writer sites; the reviewed writer currently calls `wave.open` directly. — Evidence: `"and isinstance(node.func, ast.Attribute)"`, `"and _is_wave(node.func.value)"`; `_wave_imports` handles only `"ast.Import"` and `"ast.ImportFrom"` (`test_shadow_exits.py:717–724`). Recommendation: Fix-now — Detect or explicitly refuse indirect writer references and dynamic wave imports, with mutation cases proving the combined guards reject them. Executor verification: surface=test-harness, rec=Fix-now — confirmed: `_wav_writers` matched only CALLS of `wave.open` / `Wave_write`, so `o = wave.open` then `o(p, "wb")` escaped, and `_wave_imports` saw only import statements. /fix decision: Applied. /fix notes: `_wav_writers` now counts every REFERENCE to `wave`'s `open` / `Wave_write` that is not the function of a call in a literal read mode (stored, passed or called alike) and every bare `wave` name (assigned, handed to `getattr`); `_wave_imports` adds any string equal to `"wave"` (`importlib.import_module("wave")`, `__import__("wave")`, `getattr(speech, "wave")`). Negative cases: a stored `wave.open`, a bare `wave` assigned, `getattr(wave, 'open')`, and both dynamic imports. A computed module name stays the residue named above. /fix date: 2026-10-07. /fix applied by: Claude Code (stage-3 executor).
- **PR-LOW-006** (slice C01) (LOW, behavioral, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/past_sessions.py:1087`): The review reminder stays stale when a recording crosses 365 days while the tab remains open; neither the displayed count nor “Review due.” updates automatically. — Evidence: `_tick` refreshes only under `if self._audit_failures() != self._shown_failures:`, followed by `self._render_status()`. The due count uses `now = self._clock()` at line 1030, but only when rendered. Recommendation: Fix-now — Refresh the reminder when its due state changes; test advancing an injected clock across the boundary with the tab open. Executor verification: surface=production, rec=Fix-now — confirmed: `_tick` re-rendered only on a changed audit-failure count, so the due count and "Review due." waited for the next refresh. /fix decision: Applied. /fix notes: `_review_state()` (the due count and the selected entry's due flag, from the listing in memory — no decryption) is recorded at every `_render_status` and compared by `_tick`, which re-renders when it changes; `status_lines` reads its count from the same function. Test: `test_a_review_falling_due_while_the_tab_is_open_is_shown_by_the_tick`. /fix date: 2026-10-07. /fix applied by: Claude Code (stage-3 executor).

### Round 24 - 2026-10-07 - Phase 3 (Tasks 3.1–3.4), independent cross-family codex peer-review (pass stage-3.p1, peer round 2 of cap 5)

- Round status: Closed by the stage-3 executor 2026-10-07 (2 applied — PR-MED-001, PR-MED-002; 0 pending) — the fixes' verification (targeted pytest) is composer-run, requested 2026-10-07; a confirmation peer round follows

- Source: Codex peer-review (`gpt-6-astra`, reasoning medium, read-only sandbox; three file-scoped slices; composer-recorded from `.cursor/loops/stage-3-peer-r24A.log`, `stage-3-peer-r24B.log` and `stage-3-peer-r24C.log` by the `/execute-loop` composer; escape check clean, refs unchanged)

Attack-the-class re-review of round 23: PR-MED-001 is class R23-1 (check-then-use) at the delete side — the `.part` identity check and its unlink resolve the path separately; PR-MED-002 is class R23-2 (pin escape) through the `speech.wave` module attribute.

#### Findings

- **PR-MED-001** (slice A01) (MED, behavioral, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/past_sessions.py:1876`): Recovery and failed-export cleanup can delete a replacement file because identity verification and deletion resolve the pathname separately. Another process can replace the file or its parent directory after `lstat`; the subsequent unlink deletes the replacement and recovery drops the row. This is separate from the accepted check-to-create residue. — Evidence: line 1866 reads `status = os.lstat(path)`; lines 1871–1875 compare its identity; line 1876 executes `return _unlink_quietly(path)`, which calls `path.unlink(missing_ok=True)` at line 1892. Recommendation: Fix-now — Verify identity and delete through the same Windows file handle, retaining the row when ownership cannot be secured; add a replacement-between-check-and-delete test. Executor verification: surface=production, rec=Fix-now — confirmed: `_remove_part` read the identity by path (`os.lstat`) and deleted by path (`_unlink_quietly` → `path.unlink`), so a file put at the path between the two was deleted and the row dropped; round 20 LOW-011 had named this as a same-user residue, but a same-handle delete is achievable with the standard library, so the residue is closed rather than accepted. /fix decision: Applied. /fix notes: ONE seam, `PartKernel` (`open` / `status` / `mark_deleted` / `close`), resolved at call time through `past_sessions.part_kernel()`; the real `Win32PartKernel` opens the `.part` ONCE with `_winapi.CreateFile` — `DELETE | FILE_READ_ATTRIBUTES`, share mode 0 (no other open, rename or delete while it is held), `FILE_FLAG_OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` (a link opened as itself) — hands it to an `msvcrt` descriptor, reads its identity with `os.fstat` (the same computation the export recorded from its own descriptor) and, only for a regular, non-reparse file with that identity, marks THAT handle for deletion (`SetFileInformationByHandle(FileDispositionInfo)` through `ctypes`), the file going as the handle closes. Not found (the file or its folder) is gone, as before; anything that does not secure the file — another program holding it, an identity or type that differs, a refused mark, a close that fails — closes the handle unmarked (or reports the close's failure) and KEEPS the row, so the start-up line names the file. Tests (C6 — no real Windows API in any test): the conftest pins `part_kernel` to `FsPartKernel`, a path-based double with `refuse_open` / `refuse_delete` / `swap_after_status`; `TestRemovePart` drives every decision both ways with a scripted kernel (own file marked then closed; another file, another volume, a symlink, a folder, a reparse point each closed unmarked; absent gone; a refused open, a refused mark and a failed close kept; the real kernel never built in a test); `test_a_file_swapped_in_after_the_check_is_never_deleted` and `test_a_part_another_program_holds_open_keeps_its_row` in recovery; the two `_unlink_quietly` monkeypatches replaced by `refuse_delete`. The real `Win32PartKernel` itself is exercised only on the practitioner's computer (live smoke step added). Residue (Task 5.3a): the two by-name removals of a `.part` the export created an instant before — when its stream could not be opened, or its identity could not be read — where no identity exists to check. /fix date: 2026-10-07. /fix applied by: Claude Code (stage-3 executor).
- **PR-MED-002** (slice B01) (MED, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_shadow_exits.py:682`): The WAV guards miss acquiring the existing module through the `speech.wave` attribute, storing it under another name, then opening a writer through that name. Importing `speech` adds no guarded import; neither attribute acquisition nor the subsequent aliased call changes the counters. This is reachable through `speech.py:33`’s existing module attribute, not a demonstrated current export leak or a computed-name residue. — Evidence: the bare-module branch requires `"isinstance(node, ast.Name)"` and `"node.id == \"wave\""`; the attribute branch requires `"node.attr in (\"open\", \"Wave_write\")"` (lines 677–684). `_wave_imports` checks imports and literal strings, not attribute acquisition (lines 735–752). Recommendation: Fix-now — Count acquisition of a module attribute named `wave`, and add a mutation proving the combined guards reject storing that module and subsequently writing through its alias. Executor verification: surface=test-harness, rec=Fix-now — confirmed: `w = speech.wave; w.open(p, "wb")` changed neither `_wav_writers` (the bare-module branch needed a `Name`, the call branch a `.open` on `wave`) nor `_wave_imports`. /fix decision: Applied. /fix notes: `_wav_writers` is now ONE rule over every reference to the `wave` module — the bare name or `<x>.wave` through any module — counted as a writer site UNLESS used on the spot for something that cannot write (`wave.open(..., "r"|"rb")` called there, or an attribute other than `open` / `Wave_write`, such as `wave.Error`); a reference stored, passed, returned or handed to `getattr` counts where it is taken. Negative case: the peer's `speech.wave` alias written through; `_wave_imports` also refuses `vars(speech)['wave']` (the `"wave"` string); a positive case shows a read and `wave.Error` stay uncounted, so the rule is not simply "any mention". The run-time-name residue is named in the docstring (Task 5.3a, as in round 23). /fix date: 2026-10-07. /fix applied by: Claude Code (stage-3 executor).

### Round 26 - 2026-10-07 - Phase 3 (Tasks 3.1–3.4), independent cross-family codex peer-review (pass stage-3.p1, peer round 4 of cap 5)

- Round status: Closed by the stage-3 executor 2026-10-07 (2 applied — PR-MED-001, PR-MED-002, by ONE structural change; 0 pending) — the fix's verification (`test_shadow_exits.py`) is composer-run, requested 2026-10-07

This round removes that class's SOURCE rather than its fifth shape: the `wave` pin has NO exemptions left to escape through (every reference counts, the package's three sites pinned by count), and its docstring now states what it is — a tripwire against an ACCIDENTAL second writer, not a sandbox against deliberately obfuscated code in the package — with its residue named for Task 5.3a. What converges round 5: no CRIT / HIGH / MED in the production slices, and nothing in the pins beyond that named residue (an in-place edit that keeps a count, a name built at run time); any further pin finding of that kind is Accept-with-record under D9, not a sixth patch. Round 5 is the cap — the pass closes there either way.
- Source: Codex peer-review (`gpt-6-astra`, reasoning medium, read-only sandbox; three file-scoped slices; composer-recorded from `.cursor/loops/stage-3-peer-r26A.log`, `stage-3-peer-r26B.log` and `stage-3-peer-r26C.log` by the `/execute-loop` composer; escape check clean, refs unchanged)
- Review baseline: `756b17f` + the uncommitted Phase 3 working tree (after peer round 25's fix; `test_shadow_exits.py` 84 passed, composer-run; the last wider run was round 24's 8-file selection, 1210 passed / 9 skipped)
- Slice A (the store) — 4 claims checked, 0 confirmed, 4 dropped
- Slice B (the one WAV writer, its second caller, the exit pins) — 6 claims checked, 2 confirmed, 4 dropped
- Slice C (the Past sessions tab UI) — 3 claims checked, 0 confirmed, 3 dropped

Both are class R23-2 (pin escape), its fourth and fifth instances, both test-harness, both planted-site escapes with no current leak demonstrated: PR-MED-001 a starred argument shifting which AST argument the read-mode exemption reads as the mode; PR-MED-002 the exempt `Wave_read` class reaching the `wave` namespace through its methods' `__globals__`. Slices A and C (all production code) are clean for the second round running.

#### Findings

- **PR-MED-001** (slice B01) (MED, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_shadow_exits.py:645`): Starred positional arguments can make a write-mode call appear to use a literal read mode. With an empty starred argument before a literal read-mode string, that string becomes the filename at runtime; an explicit write-mode keyword supplies the actual mode. The guard instead treats the second AST argument as the mode and exempts the writer. Reachable through the existing `speech.py:33` import; no current leak demonstrated. — Evidence: `if len(call.args) > 1: return call.args[1]`; lines 687–689 exempt the resulting literal. Recommendation: Fix-now — Refuse the read exemption for starred arguments or ambiguous argument binding, and add a mutation covering this shape. Executor verification: surface=test-harness, rec=Fix-now — confirmed: `_wave_mode` took `call.args[1]` as the mode, so `wave.open(p, *(), "rb", mode="wb")` read as a read and was exempted. The class (R23-2) has produced a new escape in each of rounds 23–26, every one through an EXEMPTION; ONE simpler rule removes the exemptions themselves without moving any production code, so it is applied once rather than patching this shape. /fix decision: Applied (with PR-MED-002, one change). /fix notes: `_wav_writers` now counts EVERY reference to the `wave` module — the bare name or `<x>.wave` through any module, called, stored, passed, introspected, reading or writing — with no exemptions; `_WAVE_REFERENCES` pins today's three sites by count (`speech.write_wav` 1 — THE writer; `benchmark.generate_speech_sample` 1 — a read of its own synthesized WAV; `speaker_eval.read_wav_pcm` 2 — the read and `wave.Error`), so a new reference anywhere, including inside those three, fails. The genuine read sites were NOT moved behind a reader in `speech.py`: pinning them by count closes the class equally without touching the measurement harness's production code (outside Phase 3). `_wave_mode` and `_WAVE_HARMLESS_ATTRIBUTES` removed. Mutation cases: the starred-argument shape at both read sites, plus all earlier ones; round 24's "a read is not a writer" positive case inverted to `test_a_new_reader_counts_too` (a genuine new reader updates the pin in the same change, where review sees it). The pin's comment and the module docstring now say it is a tripwire against an accidental second writer, not a sandbox against deliberately obfuscated code in the package; residue named for Task 5.3a: an in-place edit of a pinned site that keeps its count (`"rb"` → `"wb"` at a reader — a diff review reads) and a name built at run time. /fix date: 2026-10-07. /fix applied by: Claude Code (stage-3 executor).
- **PR-MED-002** (slice B02) (MED, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_shadow_exits.py:696`): The exempt `Wave_read` class still exposes the wave namespace through its Python methods’ `__globals__`, allowing retrieval of the writer by a literal name without changing either guard. The matcher checks only the immediate attribute, so subsequent introspection remains invisible. Reachable through the existing wave import; this is a planted-site escape, not a demonstrated current recording leak or computed-name residue. — Evidence: line 662 allows `frozenset({"Error", "Wave_read"})`; lines 696–700 exempt any load of those attributes with `continue`. Recommendation: Fix-now — Restrict exemptions to necessary contexts and reject introspection or stored references through exempt objects; add a mutation covering this route. Executor verification: surface=test-harness, rec=Fix-now — confirmed: a load of `wave.Wave_read` was exempt, and `wave.Wave_read.__init__.__globals__['open']` reaches the writer from there. /fix decision: Applied (with PR-MED-001, one change — see its notes: no exemptions remain, so `Wave_read` is no longer an exempt object). Mutation case: `wave.Wave_read.__init__.__globals__['open'](p, 'wb')` at a pinned read site. /fix date: 2026-10-07. /fix applied by: Claude Code (stage-3 executor).

No production finding is open. Trajectory 6 → 2 → 1 → 2 → 1 (LOW).
- Source: Codex peer-review (`gpt-6-astra`, reasoning medium, read-only sandbox; three file-scoped slices; composer-recorded from `.cursor/loops/stage-3-peer-r27A.log`, `stage-3-peer-r27B.log` and `stage-3-peer-r27C.log` by the `/execute-loop` composer; escape check clean, refs unchanged)

Confirmation round at the cap: no production finding; the one LOW is test isolation — three recovery tests call `monkeypatch.undo()`, which also removes the conftest's injected fake `.part` kernel, so `recover_exports()` then reaches the real Windows kernel (`Win32PartKernel`).

#### Findings

- **PR-LOW-001** (slice A01) (LOW, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_past_sessions.py:2885`): Three recovery tests remove the shared fake-kernel fixture and subsequently invoke the real Windows API, violating their isolation contract. — Evidence: `monkeypatch.undo()` precedes `recover_exports()` at lines 2885–2887, 3107–3110 and 3128–3131. The fixture uses the same monkeypatch: `monkeypatch.setattr(past_sessions, "part_kernel", lambda: kernel)` (`desktop/tests/conftest.py:304`); undo restores `return Win32PartKernel()` (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/past_sessions.py:1943`). Recommendation: Fix-now — Scope each test’s temporary patches with `monkeypatch.context()` so recovery retains the injected kernel. Executor verification: surface=test-harness, rec=Fix-now — confirmed: `test_a_part_that_cannot_be_removed_keeps_its_row_for_the_next_start`, `test_a_row_that_cannot_be_dropped_after_the_rename_is_resolved_later` and `test_a_recovery_that_cannot_be_recorded_is_resolved_again` each called `monkeypatch.undo()`, which lifted the autouse `part_kernel` fixture with the test's own patches, so the following `recover_exports()` ran `Win32PartKernel` against the test's `tmp_path` — a C6 breach in the tests, not production code. (Incidental evidence, not a test of record: those three passed in every composer run since round 24, so the real same-handle kernel opened, verified and removed a `.part` file on the dev machine.) /fix decision: Applied. /fix notes: the three tests scope their temporary patches with `with monkeypatch.context() as patched:` and no longer call `undo()`. Taken further, as the composer asked, in the existing sentinel pattern: `conftest.pytest_configure` replaces `past_sessions.part_kernel` for the whole run with `_no_real_part_kernel`, which raises `AssertionError` naming C6 (the run-wide pin pattern of `install_layout.channel` / `is_frozen`, so an `undo()` restores the REFUSAL, never the real seam — `_remove_part` calls `part_kernel()` outside its `try`, so it propagates loudly); the real seam is kept as `conftest.REAL_PART_KERNEL`. New tests: `test_lifting_the_fixture_refuses_rather_than_reaching_windows` (after `monkeypatch.undo()`, both `part_kernel()` and `_remove_part` raise) and `test_the_real_seam_builds_the_windows_kernel` (constructs only — `Win32PartKernel` has no constructor; no method runs). ruff clean; mypy 63 files clean. /fix date: 2026-10-07. /fix applied by: Claude Code (stage-3 executor).

Tests: `TestKeptSessionIds` (no unwrap at all; missing → empty, unlistable → `unreadable`), the full run's counted unwraps equal the replayed id only, and the listing refusal through `main`. /fix date: 2026-10-08. /fix applied by: Claude Code (stage-4 executor).
- **LOW-001** (`replay_kept.py` `_parser`): argparse's own error repeats what it could not parse — an unquoted `--only Jane Citizen` echoed `Citizen` (C6). /fix decision: Applied — `_QuietParser.error` prints the usage line and one fixed line ("[refused] the command line was not understood - see --help"), exit 2; `test_a_command_line_it_cannot_parse_is_never_echoed`.
- **LOW-002** (`replay_kept.py` `_run`): an unusable `--enrolment` WAV printed `read_wav_pcm`'s text, which names the file (the practitioner's own file name could be anything). /fix decision: Applied — a `WavFormatError` prints its type and the fixed `WAV_FORMAT_HELP`; `EnrolmentError`'s numbers-only text kept; `test_an_unusable_enrolment_wav_names_the_format_not_the_file`.
- **LOW-003** (`replay_kept.py` `_run`): the models refusals printed the models folder's path and the Ctrl+C line printed `gettempdir()` — paths beyond C6's one named exception. /fix decision: Applied — "in the developer build's models folder: <remedy>" and "(%TEMP%)"; `models_root()` is located, never printed; `test_a_missing_model_is_refused_without_a_path` (both models).
- **LOW-004** (`validation.py` `_Alignment` / `align_transcripts`): `align_transcripts` built `_Alignment`'s backward table too, which it never reads — double the O(n·m) memory for a long consultation. /fix decision: Applied — module functions `_forward_table` (used by `_Alignment.__init__`) and `_one_path` (the deterministic backtrace); `align_transcripts` builds the forward table only; `_Alignment.one_path` removed.

- **MED-002** (MED, test-coverage, same file): nothing proved the note leg is handed the NEW transcript — `note_drift(entry.transcript, …)` passed every test. /fix decision: Applied — the same test spies `note_drift`: one call, the document's model is the new provider's (`scripted`), clinician unresolved.

- **LOW-002** (`replay_kept.py` `_run`): the Whisper-not-installed refusal printed a shape-checked `--model` value (`--model JaneCitizen`). /fix decision: Applied — the default is named, any other "named by --model"; a new parametrized case.

- Review baseline: `ef11fcd` + the uncommitted Phase 4 working tree (FULL desktop suite 6824 passed / 10 skipped with `-rs` — the 9 symlink baseline and the environmental launcher skip; ruff clean, mypy 64 files — composer-run)
- Round classification: 🆕 new sites of rounds 29–30's classes — D: MED-001–MED-004, LOW-005–LOW-009; C: LOW-001–LOW-004, LOW-010, LOW-011 (round 30 LOW-013 and LOW-014's class at the module docstring and the launcher; round 30 LOW-015's at Task 4.1a); none fix-induced in code (round 30's `word_errors` change, `read_replay_inputs` error contract and moved `--enrolment` check checked and correct). Dropped at verification: uncapped `transcript.enc` / `note.enc` reads (as `read_entry`; a linked-in file fails authentication under the entry key), `read_replay_inputs` mapping everything to `unreadable` (its inner readers never raise `PastSessionError`), `read_note`'s second transcript decrypt (its digest check), the folder printed in "cannot be listed" / "not a Past-sessions folder" (the folder given, already a folder — C6 allows it; tightening is the practitioner's call), UNC paths for the folder or `--enrolment` (the archive's own stores admit them by design; the tool opens no connection of its own), a network `%TEMP%` (ciphertext under a memory-only key), Ctrl+C in the short pre-lock steps (a source-only traceback), `acquire()` raising (nothing held), other `SpeakerEvalError` subclasses (unreachable on this path), import order vs the offline switches (ML imports are lazy), the round-30 agreement formula and refusal-order and scan tests (sound), the listing-exclusion test (the zeroed and absent keys do read as "gone").

#### Findings

- **MED-001** (MED, test-coverage, `past_sessions.py` `read_replay_inputs`): never tested directly — its no-saved-note branch (a real case: a kept entry completed without Save), its three reasons and the key's destruction were unpinned (dropping the `_exists(note)` check survived). /fix decision: Applied — `TestReadReplayInputs` (transcript + saved note, and None without one; `not_found` / `pending` / `unreadable` for a damaged transcript and for a key that does not unwrap; the entry key destroyed on success and failure, and never unwrapped for an absent or pending id) and a full run with no saved note (the row's "Sections missing" is `-`).
- **MED-002** (MED, test-coverage, `test_replay_kept.py`): round 30's pairing test had ONE kept speaker, so reading the kept label at the NEW index (`kept_labels[j]`) survived. /fix decision: Applied — parametrized with a second case whose kept speakers match the new ones and whose extra kept word sits early: agreement 1.000 only when the kept label is read at the kept index (the mutation reads 11/12).
- **MED-003** (MED, test-coverage, same file): nothing proved `--enrolment` makes the note from the ENROLLED document (`document = plain` survived — both documents carry the same labels). /fix decision: Applied — the enrolment test spies `note_drift`: the document names an enrolled speaker and the clinician is that speaker.
- **MED-004** (MED, test-coverage, same file): "holds the exclusion for the whole run" was unpinned — releasing it right after the state check (and dropping the `finally`) still released once. /fix decision: Applied — the `_Seams` load and unwrap seams assert the exclusion is NOT yet released while the run works.
- **LOW-005** (same file): "never decrypts the label or the generated note" was only partly pinned (`_decode_label` returns None rather than raising; the fixtures write no generated note). /fix decision: Applied — in `TestReadReplayInputs`, `_decode_label` and `read_generated` are made to raise and the read succeeds.
- **LOW-006** (`tests/test_validation_metrics.py` `TestWordErrors`): `word_errors`' argument order was unpinned (all cases 4 against 4). /fix decision: Applied — `test_the_script_is_the_reference_and_the_transcript_the_hypothesis` (4 against 3 → `WordErrors(4, 3, 1)`).
- **LOW-007** (`TestTranscriptWer`): the deletion-over-insertion preference was unpinned (round 30's cases exercised diagonal-first only). /fix decision: Applied — `["a","b","a"]→["b","a","b"]` gives `((0, 1), (1, 2))` (insertion-first gives `((1, 0), (2, 1))`; traced by hand).
- **LOW-008** (`TestNoteDrift`): `prefill_id=first_detected_prefill(...)` was unpinned (the shipped defaults detect none). /fix decision: Applied — `test_the_detected_prefill_is_passed_to_the_draft` (a stand-in detector's result reaches `compose_draft`).
- **LOW-009** (`TestMain`): `--only` de-duplication was unpinned. /fix decision: Applied — `test_a_repeated_only_id_is_counted_once` (one `[run ]`, one `[skip]`, "1 skipped").
- **LOW-001** (plan Task 4.1a): still named `read_entry` and called `transcript_wer` "over `_Alignment`". /fix decision: Applied — an AS-BUILT note (the two readers, `align_transcripts` over the forward table).
- **LOW-002** (`replay_kept.py` module docstring and a comment): said the run stops only when a store "cannot be shown gone" (it also stops when the store is gone but a teardown step failed). /fix decision: Applied.
- **LOW-003** (plan C6, the module docstring): said ONE exception's text is printed (the custody line); an `EnrolmentError`'s numbers-only text is printed too. /fix decision: Applied — C6 AS-BUILT note, the docstring and the print site name it and the rule for a new raiser.
- **LOW-004** (`kept-recordings.md`): "Listing the entries opens nothing" — it reads each entry's audio-key file for the zero-check. /fix decision: Applied — "decrypts nothing (it reads the folder names and each entry's audio-key file, only to see whether it was zeroed)".
- **LOW-010** (`scripts/replay-kept-recordings.py`, `scripts/README.md`, AGENTS.md step 13): the launcher's examples were unlabelled Command Prompt syntax (round 30 LOW-014's class), and all three said the output is "session ids and (DRIFT) numbers only" (it also prints model names and the folder given). /fix decision: Applied.
- **LOW-011** (AGENTS.md "If touching Past sessions" pointer): did not name the replay tool's two readers or D11 — `read_replay_inputs` looks like a near-duplicate of `read_entry` to a later simplifier. /fix decision: Applied — one clause naming both readers and that neither may be folded into `list_entries` / `read_entry`.

#### Findings

- **MED-001** (MED, overclaim, `docs/practice/development-recording-consent.md` Part A, `privacy-information.md` audio row): "will not save that copy to an online or shared storage folder (such as OneDrive)" / "refuses synced, shared and removable places" — `exclusions.check_export_location` recognises only OneDrive's roots, network paths, the roaming profile, non-fixed drives and the app folders (Dropbox, Google Drive or a USB SSD reported as fixed pass). /fix decision: Applied — "refuses OneDrive, a network drive or a removable drive; your practitioner makes sure it is in no other synced, shared or backed-up folder".

- **MED-003** (MED, README rule, the consent document's "How long it is kept" and the audio rows of the patient sheet and privacy information): a record-keeping rule stated with no state citation or `[Reviewer: …]` marker; the banner lacked the state-coverage clause. /fix decision: Applied — a `[Reviewer: …]` marker citing VIC/NSW/ACT (7 years, or 25 for a child) and APP 11.2 on each of the three; the banner clause added.
- **MED-004** (MED, procedure vs code, the consent document Part B steps 2–3): the second tick binds to the note Chrome's focused tab reports AT TICK TIME (`ui/session_screen._on_development_toggled` → `ChromeBridge._development_target`) and clears on any other report of the bound tab (`_check_development_target`), so "tick on the Session tab, then press Start in Chrome" loses the tick when the note is opened after ticking; the dropped-link clear, the side panel's own consent tick and the `getting_ready` exception were unstated. /fix decision: Applied — separate desktop and Chrome procedures (open the note in Chrome first, then tick, then consent and Start in the panel) and the full clear list.
- **MED-005** (MED, wrong order, threat model "Kept recordings" EXPORT): the custody order put the shadow question first and the ledger resolution after the store's re-check. Code: tab — layer, dialog, `check_export_location`, question (`ui/past_sessions.py:781-801`); store — relative folder, `realpath`, ledger, `<id>.wav`, `check_destination`, exclusive create (`past_sessions.py:1212-1260`). /fix decision: Applied — the paragraph rewritten in the code's order.
- **LOW-006** ("exported once" / "one unencrypted copy" in the threat model, data-flow map, PLAN.md, the pilot README, `speaker-measurement.md`, the plan-pilot reconciliation and the practice documents): no once-only limit exists — only an existing `<id>.wav` in the chosen folder refuses, and `exports` is a count. /fix decision: Applied — "each Export writes one `<session id>.wav`; the audit row counts them" in the security documents; "an unencrypted copy" elsewhere; Part B states keeping at most one at a time as the practitioner's practice. - **LOW-007** (threat model, the second consent): "the setting going off clears it, and the Session tab says so" — that clear is unannounced (`session_screen.py:308`). /fix decision: Applied.

- **LOW-011** (data-flow flow 27, retention row): the review status line COUNTS kept recordings due (`review_due_line`), it names none; the opened entry says "Review due.". /fix decision: Applied.
- **LOW-012** (threat model, THE ONE WRITER): `_WAVE_REFERENCES` counts `wave`; `write_wav` / `wav_bytes` are `_WRITER_REFERENCES`. /fix decision: Applied.
- **LOW-013** (threat model, THE REPLAY TOOL OUTPUT): the folder given on the command line is printed too (`replay_kept.py` refusals and the report header). /fix decision: Applied.
- **LOW-014** (`pilot-builds.md` rollback step 2, threat-model residue (17), flow 27): a busy or reset ledger, or a `.part` on a drive not attached, is resolved by nothing the start-up can name; a failure before the identity is read removes the `.part` by name. /fix decision: Applied — the by-hand search covers those cases; flow 27 and the EXPORT paragraph say "by its name if no identity was read yet".

- **LOW-008** (`docs/practice/README.md` open questions): round 35 LOW-010's marker pointed at a question the README did not hold (telling the patient the program may be offered to other practitioners). /fix decision: Applied — the bullet added.
- **LOW-009** (`privacy-information.md` "How it is protected"; the patient sheet's matching bullet): "Everything the program keeps … is encrypted" beside the disclosed unencrypted copy. /fix decision: Applied — the exception named in both.
- **LOW-010** (`retention-schedule.md` "`pre_audit` rows", pre-existing since Phase 2): the reconciled commit dated "by now" and expiry "by its completion" — the unattended writers date by the session's start and skip a month past its prune date (`past_sessions_view.unattended_write`, `:617-653`). /fix decision: Applied — the deliberate vs unattended rule, as the threat model states it.
- **LOW-011** (threat model THE AUDIT FACTS): "a month the 7-year prune removed" — the rule judges the month's prune DATE, whether or not the prune has run (`audit.keeps_rows_of`). /fix decision: Applied.
- **LOW-012** (threat model residue (5)): "the ledger row is already gone" — a kill between the rename and the row's drop leaves the row, dropped at the next start as resolved without a count (`past_sessions.py:1311`; `_remove_part` on a missing file). /fix decision: Applied — both windows named.

### Round 37 - 2026-10-08 - Phase 5 (Tasks 5.1, 5.2, 5.3a, 5.3b), independent cross-family codex peer-review (pass stage-5.p1, peer round 1 of cap 5)

- Round status: Closed by the stage-5 executor 2026-10-08 (5 verified and applied, 0 invalid; 0 pending) — documents only, NO production change (PR-MED-003 verified a misstated residue, not a code defect); `docs/pilot/pilot-log-template.md` touched, so the doc-pinning tests are owed (composer-run)
- Source: Codex peer-review (`gpt-6-astra`, reasoning medium, read-only sandbox; three file-scoped slices; composer-recorded from `.cursor/loops/stage-5-peer-r37A.log`, `stage-5-peer-r37B.log` and `stage-5-peer-r37C.log` by the `/execute-loop` composer; escape check clean, refs unchanged)
- Review baseline: `e3a9b9f` + the uncommitted Phase 5 working tree (after review-loop rounds 34–36, accept-closed at the cap; FULL desktop suite 6833 passed / 9 skipped after round 34; doc-pinning tests 115 passed after round 36, composer-run)
- Slice A (the patient- and practitioner-facing practice and pilot documents) — 3 claims checked, 2 confirmed, 1 dropped
- Slice B (the threat model's Kept recordings section and the data-flow map) — 34 claims checked, 32 confirmed accurate, 0 dropped; 2 defects
- Slice C (retention, intended use, incident process, release rollback rule, PLAN.md, AGENTS.md, CHANGELOG, testing docs, sibling plans) — 1 claim checked, 1 confirmed, 0 dropped

PR-MED-003 names a code ordering (Delete now removes the entry before its `deleted_early` audit write, and the start-up repair cannot recover a deletion fact once the whole entry is gone) that the threat model's repair claim overstates — the executor verifies whether it is a residue to state or a code fix.

#### Findings

- **PR-MED-001** (slice A01) (MED, docs-only, `docs/practice/development-recording-consent.md:189`): The annual-review procedure promises a reminder that unreadable entries never receive, leaving those kept recordings outside the described review procedure. — Evidence: “the Past sessions tab says so” and “the session's own line says ‘Review due.’”; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/past_sessions_view.py:414` returns `False` when the label is unavailable, and line 439 shows only “Recording kept for development.” The same blanket promise appears in `docs/pilot/pilot-log-template.md:67`. Recommendation: Fix-now — Qualify both descriptions and give an explicit review/deletion procedure for unreadable kept entries; describe the reminder as 365 days after completion. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — review_due returns False for label None (past_sessions_view.py:414) and recording_line shows no review month (:438-439); REVIEW_AFTER is 365 days from completed_at (:391, :416)`. /fix decision: Applied — the consent document's "The 12-month review" says "a year (365 days) after you pressed Complete" and adds a by-hand review of every "Cannot be read on this Windows account" row (delete it if whose or why cannot be told); the pilot-log template's intro says the same and points there; the data-flow map's flow 27 and the retention schedule's "Kept recording" row say an unreadable entry is never counted or marked.
- **PR-LOW-002** (slice A02) (LOW, docs-only, `docs/pilot/pilot-log-template.md:73`): The review table cannot distinguish multiple recordings kept outside the pilot log on the same day, so a later reviewer cannot reliably associate a retention reason or last-review date with its recording. — Evidence: “`—` for a recording kept outside the pilot log (then the next column identifies it)” followed by “the consultation's date (YYYY-MM-DD)”; `docs/practice/development-recording-consent.md:193` requires reviewing again “12 months after its last review row.” Recommendation: Fix-now — Record the displayed consultation date and time for these entries, with a content-free disambiguation rule, and update the table pins if its columns change. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — entry_line shows "YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM - <who>" (past_sessions_view.py:352, :381), so the minute is the tab's own content-free key`. /fix decision: Applied — `Consulted` is the date and time exactly as the tab's row shows it; two recordings showing the same minute are reviewed together with one outcome; `Row` also takes `unreadable` (with `Consulted` `—`); the retention schedule's "Filled pilot log" row matches. No column added or renamed, so `test_pilot_docs.LOG_REVIEW_COLUMNS` is unchanged (the selection below re-checks the template).
- **PR-MED-003** (slice B01) (MED, docs-only, `docs/security/threat-model.md:3723`): The audit residue overstates repair: Delete now can permanently lose a recording’s deletion fact when its audit update fails after the entry is removed. — Evidence: “the start-up repair closes the kept and deleted facts from the files”; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/past_sessions.py:657` calls `store.delete_entry(session_id)` before `record_past_session(..., "deleted_early", ...)` at line 671, while `app.py:529` repairs only entries returned by `past_sessions.deleted_recordings()`. Recommendation: Fix-now — Limit the repair claim to surviving evidence and explicitly retain the unrecoverable audit gap after whole-entry deletion. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — the ordering is the code's deliberate contract, not a defect`: Delete now records the KEPT fact (and an already-deleted recording's deletion) BEFORE `delete_entry` (`ui/past_sessions.py:643-657`), and the retention sweep does the same through `before_held_delete` (`past_sessions_view.py:853-857`) — Phase 2's "the kept fact first, before any destroyer acts" holds; the `deleted_at` that `deleted_early` / `expired` set is written only AFTER the entry is gone (`ui/past_sessions.py:671`, `past_sessions_view.py:860-865`) because it is true only then — writing it first would record a deletion that a failed `delete_entry` (`:658-660`) never made, a false "destroyed" fact, worse than a missing one. A failed write there is residue (4)'s own C2 case (never raised, counted), but no file survives for the repair. Not production; no scope question for Hardening. /fix decision: Applied — residue (4) limits the repair to surviving evidence (a committed kept entry; a deleted recording's zeroed key or held-back files) and names the whole-entry gap (`kept_at` without `deleted_at` for good; an audit gap, never a custody one); the CHANGELOG's Phase 2 sentence says the same.
- **PR-LOW-004** (slice B02) (LOW, docs-only, `docs/security/data-flow-map.md:1435`): The non-flow excludes the replay tool’s temporary encrypted copy, contradicting flow 27 and D11. — Evidence: “never a copy the app makes elsewhere except the practitioner's own Export”; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:1110` creates `temp_root`, and line 1116 calls `_write_store(..., require_key=persist_key)` for replay’s memory-keyed temporary store. Recommendation: Fix-now — Name replay’s temporary encrypted store as another exception, retaining its memory-only key and teardown qualifications. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — speaker_eval.transcribe_in_temporary_store mkdtemp's a root (speaker_eval.py:1110) and writes the PCM there re-encrypted (:1116) with no key file under persist_key=False (:1114-1115)`. /fix decision: Applied — the non-flow names the replay tool's temporary store as the second exception: one entry at a time under `%TEMP%`, a key held only in memory and never written, its key destroyed then its folder removed on every path (flow 27, REPLAY).
- **PR-MED-005** (slice C01) (MED, docs-only, `docs/release/pilot-builds.md:75`): The rollback guidance offers whole-entry deletion for development-consent withdrawal, contradicting the requirement to retain the transcript and notes. — Evidence: “withdrawal needs 0.3.0's Delete recording (or Delete now of the whole entry, which also deletes the transcript and notes).” However, `docs/security/retention-schedule.md:120` says “while the transcript and notes stay”; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/past_sessions_view.py:180` says “Use Delete now only for a recording made in error”. Plan D8 preserves that restriction. Recommendation: Fix-now — Remove Delete now as a withdrawal alternative; instruct the practitioner to use 0.3.0 or later’s Delete recording, preserving the transcript and notes. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — DELETE_HELP "Use Delete now only for a recording made in error" (past_sessions_view.py:179-182), the transcript kept 7 years minimum (privacy plan H5), and D8's withdrawal path is Delete recording`. /fix decision: Applied — `pilot-builds.md` says withdrawal needs Delete recording, so install 0.3.0 or later again to honour it the same day, and never to use Delete now for a withdrawal (only for a recording made in error; it would also delete the transcript and notes). No other document offered Delete now for a withdrawal (grep).

- Round status: Closed by the stage-5 executor 2026-10-08 (5 verified and applied, 0 invalid; 0 pending; plus the class sweep's fixes) — documents only, NO production change; `docs/pilot/` NOT touched this round, and no code changed
- Source: Codex peer-review (`gpt-6-astra`, reasoning medium, read-only sandbox; three file-scoped slices; composer-recorded from `.cursor/loops/stage-5-peer-r38A.log`, `stage-5-peer-r38B.log` and `stage-5-peer-r38C.log` by the `/execute-loop` composer; escape check clean, refs unchanged)
- Review baseline: `e3a9b9f` + the uncommitted Phase 5 working tree (after peer round 37's fixes; doc-pinning tests 115 passed, composer-run)
- Slice A (the practice and pilot documents) — 1 claim checked, 1 confirmed, 0 dropped
- Slice B (the threat model and data-flow map) — 36 claims checked, 35 confirmed accurate, 0 dropped; 1 defect
- Slice C (retention, release, PLAN.md, AGENTS.md, CHANGELOG, testing docs, sibling plans) — 4 claims checked, 3 confirmed, 1 dropped
- Peer-ID map (journal grammar): A01 → PR-MED-001, B01 → PR-MED-002, C01 → PR-MED-003, C02 → PR-MED-004, C03 → PR-MED-005. All docs-only; attack-the-class over round 37: PR-MED-001 and PR-MED-002 are class R37-1/R37-2 (a condition stated wrongly — audio held for note preparation and recovery needs no development consent; Discard keeps nothing whatever the consent); PR-MED-004 is R37-2 ("on every path" for the replay's clean-up); PR-MED-005 is round 37's own fix not carried into the CHANGELOG and PLAN.md summaries; PR-MED-003 is a rollback-procedure gap for a `.part` whose ledger row an earlier start-up dropped.

#### Findings

- **PR-MED-001** (slice A01) (MED, docs-only, `docs/practice/privacy-information.md:24`): The new wording incorrectly makes written development consent a condition of holding audio after the appointment, omitting ordinary note preparation and recovery. — Evidence: “while it is in progress (and afterwards only if you separately agree in writing to it being kept)”. `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session.py:1039–1084` seals the recording and enters processing without deleting it; deletion occurs at Complete (`session_store.py:1404`). The document’s own audio-retention row at line 109 describes temporary retention without development consent. Recommendation: Fix-now — Distinguish temporary retention while preparing or recovering the note from keeping audio after Complete for development. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — the recording is held through processing and recovery (the 24 h window) and deleted at Complete or Discard; only a consented non-mock Complete keeps it (session.py:1197 keep_audio=)`. /fix decision: Applied — the collected-audio bullet now says the recording is held while the note is prepared (up to 24 hours if interrupted), deleted when the note is finished or discarded, and kept after the note is finished only with the separate written consent.
- **PR-MED-002** (slice B01) (MED, docs-only, `docs/security/threat-model.md:3457`): The shadow-recording paragraph incorrectly extends the consent exception to Discard, implying consented audio survives that action. — Evidence: “is destroyed at Complete or Discard — unless … the patient consented in writing”. Discard removes the unfinished archive entry (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session.py:1517`) and calls `discard_session` unconditionally (`:1562`), which executes `delete_session_key(session_dir)` (`desktop/src/scribe_desktop/session_store.py:1429`). Flow 27 correctly says Discard keeps no audio (`docs/security/data-flow-map.md:1228`). Recommendation: Fix-now — Attach the consent exception only to non-mock Complete; state separately that Discard destroys the recording regardless of development consent. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — keep_audio is passed only on the Complete path (session.py:1197, the only reference); Discard and the sweep destroy unconditionally`. /fix decision: Applied — the threat model's shadow paragraph: "except that, since 0.3.0, a non-mock COMPLETE keeps it … (a Discard destroys it whatever was ticked)"; the class sweep found and fixed the same misplaced exception in `docs/testing/shipping-gate.md` L30, and added the Complete condition to the retention schedule's kept-recordings intro ("a Discard, an expired recovery store or a mock Complete keeps nothing") and `docs/testing/kept-recordings.md`'s opening line. The downtime procedure already stated it correctly.
- **PR-MED-003** (slice C01) (MED, docs-only, `docs/release/pilot-builds.md:106`): The rollback procedure misses interrupted exports whose ledger rows were lost during an earlier startup. A drive absent during that startup can be reattached before rollback; the final restart then neither removes nor reports its plaintext `.part`. — Evidence: manual inspection is conditional on a drive “not attached now”; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/past_sessions.py:2004–2005` treats `FileNotFoundError` as resolved, and lines 1338–1341 persist the reduced ledger. An earlier unreadable-ledger reset similarly forgets paths. Recommendation: Fix-now — Require checking previous export folders before rollback, including previously unavailable destinations or previously reset ledgers, regardless of current warnings. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — _remove_part returns True on FileNotFoundError (past_sessions.py:2004-2005, a missing folder or drive by design, residue (9)) and recover_exports persists the reduced ledger (:1338-1341); a reset ledger forgets every row (:1328-1331)`. Not production: dropping such rows is the documented trade (round 20 LOW-005 — a folder deleted by hand must not hold its row for ever). /fix decision: Applied — `pilot-builds.md` step 2 makes the by-hand search of every folder ever exported to (external drives attached first) ALWAYS part of the gate, whatever the tab said, and says why; residue (17) and the CHANGELOG's Phase 5 summary say the same.
- **PR-MED-004** (slice C02) (MED, docs-only, `CHANGELOG.md:8`): The CHANGELOG still promises replay cleanup on every path, although failed deletion or interrupted teardown can leave encrypted temporary files requiring manual removal. — Evidence: “removes its temporary store on every path”; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/replay_kept.py:704–706` explicitly warns that interrupted teardown may leave a folder, and `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/speaker_eval.py:979` instructs manual deletion when removal fails. Recommendation: Fix-now — Describe attempted cleanup, memory-only keys, and the diagnostic/manual-removal exception already documented in the retention schedule. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — replay_kept.py:702-707 (the interrupted line), speaker_eval.py:973-980 (a residue named for deletion by hand)`. /fix decision: Applied — the CHANGELOG says the store is torn down on every path and a folder it cannot remove, or an interrupted run leaves, is keyless ciphertext named for deletion by hand; the class sweep found the same "torn down on every path" overclaim in data-flow flow 27 (REPLAY) and in round 37's own non-flow wording, both qualified. The threat model's REPLAY TOOL paragraph already qualifies it (a store not shown gone STOPS the run).
- **PR-MED-005** (slice C03) (MED, docs-only, `CHANGELOG.md:8`): Round 37’s unreadable-entry reminder correction remains absent from the CHANGELOG and PLAN delivery summary. These still describe the dated reminder without its readability condition. — Evidence: “its pane shows ‘Recording kept for development - review due <month year>’” and `PLAN.md:181` “a review line at 12 months”; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/past_sessions_view.py:414–416` excludes unreadable labels, and lines 438–439 show only “Recording kept for development.” Recommendation: Fix-now — Qualify both summaries: the dated reminder requires a readable label and begins 365 days after Complete; unreadable entries need manual review. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — past_sessions_view.py:409-416, :438-439`. /fix decision: Applied — the CHANGELOG's Phase 3 summary (365 days after Complete; an unreadable entry never counted or marked, reviewed by hand) and PLAN.md's delivery note; the class sweep carried it also into the threat model's "Kept recordings" opening ("review reminder 365 days after its Complete …") and the retention schedule's kept-recordings intro.

PEER-ROUND-38 RESULT: 5 findings (CRIT 0 / HIGH 0 / MED 5 / LOW 0) — slice A 1, slice B 1, slice C 3. Executor verification: all 5 confirmed, `surface=docs` each, all applied; no production change. Executor class sweep (the composer's instruction, same leg) over every Phase 5 document — (a) a condition or exception stated wrongly, (b) "every path / always / never / only" overclaims, (c) a fix not carried into every summary (CHANGELOG, PLAN.md, AGENTS.md, the practice README's reviewer questions) — found and fixed, beyond the five: `shipping-gate.md` L30 (the exception on Discard); the retention schedule's kept-recordings intro and `kept-recordings.md`'s opening (the Complete condition); flow 27 REPLAY and the "no second root" non-flow ("torn down on every path"); the threat model's opening and the retention intro (the 365-day / unreadable reminder); the privacy information's as-built 0.3.0 line ("only unencrypted copy … on this computer" → the folders the program accepts, an external fixed-reported drive not refused). Checked and left: every `[Reviewer: …]` marker in the three patient documents maps to a README open question; AGENTS.md's "two steps" still holds (the search is part of step 2); the downtime procedure's Complete/Discard wording was already right.

### Round 39 - 2026-10-08 - Phase 5 (Tasks 5.1, 5.2, 5.3a, 5.3b), independent cross-family codex peer-review (pass stage-5.p1, peer round 3 of cap 5)

- Round status: Closed by the stage-5 executor 2026-10-08 (3 verified and applied, 0 invalid; 0 pending; plus the class sweep's fixes) — documents only, NO production change; `docs/pilot/` not touched, no code changed
- Source: Codex peer-review (`gpt-6-astra`, reasoning medium, read-only sandbox; three file-scoped slices; composer-recorded from `.cursor/loops/stage-5-peer-r39A.log`, `stage-5-peer-r39B.log` and `stage-5-peer-r39C.log` by the `/execute-loop` composer; escape check clean, refs unchanged)
- Review baseline: `e3a9b9f` + the uncommitted Phase 5 working tree (after peer round 38's fixes and class sweep; doc-pinning tests 115 passed, composer-run)
- Slice A (the practice and pilot documents) — 2 claims checked, 1 confirmed, 1 dropped
- Slice B (the threat model and data-flow map) — 39 claims checked, 38 confirmed accurate, 0 dropped; 1 defect
- Slice C (retention, release, PLAN.md, AGENTS.md, CHANGELOG, testing docs, sibling plans) — 1 claim checked, 1 confirmed, 0 dropped
- Peer-ID map (journal grammar): A01 → PR-HIGH-001, B01 → PR-MED-002, C01 → PR-LOW-003. All docs-only. PR-HIGH-001 is class R37-2 (an overclaim) in the patient-facing consent sheet: it promises Export always refuses OneDrive, while `check_export_location` detects only the OneDrive roots Windows reports in the environment; graded HIGH under the round's rubric (a statement that could mislead a patient into consenting). PR-MED-002 is class R37-1 (a condition omitted): recording or repairing `kept_at` needs a readable label. PR-LOW-003 is class R38-5 (a fix not carried into a summary).

#### Findings

- **PR-HIGH-001** (slice A01) (HIGH, docs-only, `docs/practice/development-recording-consent.md:61`): The consent sheet promises unconditional OneDrive export refusal, which the app cannot enforce when the destination’s OneDrive root is absent from its environment. — Evidence: “The program refuses to save that copy to OneDrive”; lines 206–212 repeat this and limit the practitioner’s sync check to software “other than OneDrive”. However, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/exclusions.py:421–424` detects only three environment-provided roots and skips missing values; `:487–502` accepts an otherwise eligible fixed-drive folder. The same overclaim appears in `docs/practice/privacy-information.md:112` and `:169`. This could induce consent based on an unsupported protection against cloud copying. Recommendation: Fix-now — Qualify refusal as covering detected locations and require the practitioner to check all sync arrangements, including OneDrive, consistently across these passages. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — check_location tests only the OneDrive, OneDriveCommercial and OneDriveConsumer variables, skipping an unset one (exclusions.py:421-425), and check_export_location then admits any other fixed-drive folder (:486-502)`; graded HIGH and kept HIGH (patient-facing consent text), applied under high-auto=on — no MUST-PAUSE. /fix decision: Applied — the consent document's Part A ("refuses … in some places it can recognise … but it cannot recognise every synced or shared folder, OneDrive included", so the practitioner checks) and Part B's Export paragraph ("a folder inside OneDrive (only where Windows tells it where OneDrive is)"; "no sync software is reliably detected, OneDrive included"); the privacy information's audio row and its 0.3.0 as-built line. The sweep carried the same qualification into `kept-recordings.md` (the three variables named), the retention schedule's "Exported recording" row, data-flow flow 27 EXPORT, threat-model residue (7) (an unset or elsewhere-pointing variable is not seen), PLAN.md's delivery note and the CHANGELOG's Phase 3 line, and into the two OLDER data-folder warning sentences of the same shape in the patient sheet ("How it is protected") and the privacy information ("Where it goes"): "a place it can recognise as synced or shared … it cannot recognise every one". No "other than OneDrive" wording remains (grep).
- **PR-MED-002** (slice B01) (MED, docs-only, `docs/security/threat-model.md:3713`): The audit description omits the readable-label requirement for recording or repairing `kept_at`. A surviving recording with an unreadable label can remain without that fact even when its audit row exists; Delete recording and Delete now also skip the kept-fact write in this case. — Evidence: “Every destroyer of a kept entry … records the kept fact first”; lines 3708–3709 also promise that a missed completion write “gets `kept_at`”. However, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/app.py:500–501` explicitly skips repair with `if label is None: continue`; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/past_sessions.py:643` and `:744` likewise require a readable label before recording the kept fact. Recommendation: Fix-now — Qualify these claims, residue (4), and flow 27’s repair summary: recovering `kept_at` requires the label’s completion time; deletion can still be recorded without it. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — app.py:500-501 (repair skips a None label), ui/past_sessions.py:643 (Delete now) and :744 (Delete recording) record kept_at only with a label; the deletion is recorded without one (:662-670; app.record_deleted_recordings on an existing row only, app.py:519-527); the retention sweep never expires an undated entry`. /fix decision: Applied — the threat model's THE AUDIT FACTS (the repair "WHEN ITS LABEL CAN BE READ"; each destroyer records the kept fact first "when the entry's label can be read", with only the deletion recorded otherwise and undated entries never expired) and residue (4); flow 27 COMPLETE (the repair needs a readable label) and PAST SESSIONS (Delete now's and expiry's `deleted_at` follows the deletion, not repaired); the CHANGELOG's Phase 2 line and AGENTS.md's Past-sessions pointer.
- **PR-LOW-003** (slice C01) (LOW, docs-only, `AGENTS.md:78`): The audit-ordering correction was not carried into the project summary. — Evidence: “the kept/deleted audit facts are recorded before any destroyer acts”; however, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/ui/past_sessions.py:657` deletes the entry before recording `deleted_early` at line 671, and `ui/past_sessions_view.py:853–865` likewise records expiry after deletion. `CHANGELOG.md:8` correctly says “the deletion once the entry is gone”. Recommendation: Fix-now — Align AGENTS.md with that distinction: existing facts precede destruction; the new deletion outcome follows it. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — ui/past_sessions.py:657/:671, past_sessions_view.py:853-865`. /fix decision: Applied — AGENTS.md's Phase 2 sentence (facts that already hold before any destroyer acts, the destroyer's own deletion after it, the whole-entry gap of residue (4), `kept_at` needs a readable label); the Current Status reviews line brought up to rounds 37–39. AGENTS.md is not test-pinned (`test_install_layout.py` reads a remedy string, not the file).

PEER-ROUND-39 RESULT: 3 findings (CRIT 0 / HIGH 1 / MED 1 / LOW 1) — slice A 1, slice B 1, slice C 1. Executor verification: all 3 confirmed, `surface=docs` each, applied (the HIGH kept HIGH and applied under high-auto=on); no production change. #### Findings

- **MED-001** (MED, privacy / docs vs code, `docs/practice/development-recording-consent.md:144–150`; code `ui/bridge.py:726–743`, `protocol.py:199–201`): Part B told the practitioner the development tick "also clears if Chrome then shows another note or patient". Chrome reports patient and note ids ONLY on a treatment-note page, so a tick armed while Chrome shows a patient page, an appointment list, another site or no link at all is bound to NO ids (`development_target is None`); every later non-note report also gives None, so moving to the NEXT patient's non-note page never clears it, and the next desktop Start — for whoever it is — consumes it (A arms, A does not go ahead, B's Start keeps B's audio). The code follows D2 (ids compared when there are ids; a note report clears a None-bound tick; a link drop clears an id-bound one); the practitioner-facing procedure overclaimed. The controls that remain: the "Next recording will be kept for development" line while armed, the Complete line, Delete recording. /fix decision: Applied (documents; the code's behaviour accepted at the executor gate as a named residue rather than a new clearing rule — a time limit or "any report clears" would be a product choice D2 did not make, and neither can tell two patients' non-note pages apart): Part B item 3 now scopes the Chrome clears to a tick given while a note was open and adds "A box ticked while Chrome shows no treatment note is tied to no patient: it stays ticked until the next Start, whoever that is for — so if that patient does not go ahead, untick it yourself."; the threat model's residue (2) names the unbound tick and its controls; flow 27 says it; `docs/design-system.md`'s cleared-tick sentence scoped; the CHANGELOG's Phase 2 line. PRACTITIONER-FACING WORDING CHANGED (joins the pending approval of `development-consent-v1`).
- **LOW-002** (LOW, docs vs code, `docs/security/threat-model.md:3652`, `data-flow-map.md:1216`, the consent document, `docs/design-system.md`, CHANGELOG; code `ui/main_window.py:937–941`, `system_events.py:295–301`): "a lock or a suspend clears it" is unconditional; the lock clears the tick only while the session-lock registration stands (`status.lock == "on"`), and sleep only while Windows reports it — the Status tab names a failed registration ("Lock pause unavailable …", "Sleep pause may not work …"). /fix decision: Applied — each restatement says "while Windows reports it" (the consent document: "unless the Status tab says lock or sleep pause is unavailable").
- **LOW-003** (LOW, custody, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/past_sessions.py` `remove_pending_entry`): H1 round 32 LOW-001's rule (a link status that cannot be read is NOT the destroyer's go-ahead) was applied to the entry and the staging copy but not to `.staging` itself — `_staging_linked()` is `_is_link`, whose "cannot tell" reads as "a link", so the staging removal was skipped and `removed` stayed True; the Discard or expiry then deleted the source key while a staging copy (since Phase 2 possibly holding a kept recording's audio, staged for seconds) stayed readable until `clean_staging` could inspect `.staging` again. Pre-existing (privacy plan), sharpened by Phase 2. /fix decision: Applied — `link_state(self.staging_root)` read once: None → False (the source key stays this time), True → the staging copy is not ours (as before), False → removed key-first; docstring extended. Test: `TestPendingLifecycle.test_a_staging_folder_whose_link_status_cannot_be_read_refuses` (an `os.lstat` that fails on `.staging` only → False with the staged key kept; once readable → True and the copy gone).
- **LOW-004** (LOW, docstring, `past_sessions.py` module docstring "Threading"): "every method runs on the GUI thread" — a Discard waiting for live transcription runs `controller.discard()` on a `TaskThread` (`ui/session_screen.py:708–740`, installation plan round 40 LOW-002), and so `remove_pending_entry`, under the controller's lock. Harmless (a live session has no entry or staging copy), but a future caller could rely on the false rule. /fix decision: Applied — the docstring names the exception and why it is safe.
- **LOW-005** (LOW, hardening, `speech.py` `write_wav`): the one plaintext-audio writer still accepts a `Path`, which creates or REPLACES a file — a mode Export's "never replaced" rule does not want; only `test_validation_set.py` uses it (both package callers pass a stream). /fix decision: No change — the signature is Task 3.3's own (`target: Path | BinaryIO`), and `_WRITER_REFERENCES` in `test_shadow_exits.py` counts every reference, so a new path-mode caller is a pin change a reviewer sees; narrowing is a refactor for H2 to weigh, not a defect.
- **LOW-006** (LOW, docs, threat model residue (9); code `past_sessions.recover_exports` → `_resolve` → `Win32PartKernel.open`): start-up recovery opens each unresolved row's recorded path without re-checking its drive; a letter that passed as fixed (a USB SSD, residue (7)) and has since been mapped to a network share sends that open to the share — Windows' own file access, no socket of the app's, contrived (a killed export plus a re-used letter). A code fix would need the `WindowsLayer` before recovery (a start-up reorder) — not worth it. /fix decision: Applied — residue (9) names it.
- **LOW-007** (LOW, docs vs code, `docs/design-system.md:638–640`; code `ui/session_screen.py:308–309`): "A tick cleared by anything but a Start that ran … is NAMED, never silent" — the setting turned off clears it unannounced (the threat model and flow 27 already said so). /fix decision: Applied — folded into MED-001's rewrite of the sentence ("or the setting being turned off (the tick is then hidden, unannounced)").
- **LOW-008** (LOW, docs vs code, `CHANGELOG.md:8`; code `ui/session_screen.py:499–503`, `517–519`): "only "still getting ready" leaves them set" — a Start refused while a Discard is under way keeps the ORDINARY consent tick (only the development tick is cleared). /fix decision: Applied — "only "still getting ready" leaves the development tick set; a Discard under way keeps the ordinary consent tick".
