- Checked and clean (by category): KEY WRAPPING — a fresh audio key per stage and per retried Complete, AAD `past-audio-key:<validated id>` used by no other artifact, random 96-bit nonces, both keys destroyed in `finally` on every reader and writer, verification through the key read back from disk with the footer required and SHA-256 compared; ZERO-THEN-UNLINK — the handle checks (regular, one link, ≤ 60 bytes), zeros flushed, synced and read back, the record before the unlinks, unlinks skipped on a failed record, tidy only for cleared ids (the path-check-then-`r+b`-open window needs a same-user process — boundary 2 — and is bounded by the handle checks); EXPORT LOCATION — every exotic path form resolves through `realpath` to a drive path or has no drive letter and is refused, ADS unreachable (folder only; the name fixed), the channels' app folders compared apart, `O_CREAT|O_EXCL`, `rename` never replaces, the ledger row before any audio, no temporary file or log line with content; LEDGER — AES-GCM with a fixed AAD (garbage fails authentication; only the two fixed names are ever reset), row keys pattern-checked, capped at 256 KiB, an authentic row deletes only `<folder>\<id>.wav.part` through the one exclusive handle with the recorded identity; TRAVERSAL — every id through `validate_session_id` or the same pattern before a path is built; REPLAY — the exclusion held and released, offline applied then asserted, label and generated note never decrypted, the memory-only key, the printed fields as documented, argparse's echo replaced; CONTENT-FREE RECORDS — every new `_log` a `detail_code` plus an optional validated id, every new `print` content-free, `RecordingRecord` datetimes and an integer, `kept_at` registered in all three renderings, the user-facing lines name `<id>.wav` / `.part` only; RESOURCE BOUNDS — 60 bytes, 256 KiB, 4 KiB, streamed audio (the replay tool's per-entry buffer is residue (14)).

#### Findings

- **SEC-001** (MED — graded up from the lens's LOW: a path OFF this computer (C2) for real consultation content that no document named; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/replay_kept.py` `_run` → `_replay_all`; run as `.venv\Scripts\python.exe scripts\replay-kept-recordings.py`): the replay tool holds one whole kept consultation's PCM, its kept transcript, saved note and new transcript in memory while CTranslate2 and onnxruntime — the process's most crash-prone native code — run; it runs under `python.exe`, which no Windows Error Reporting exclusion covers (`register-native-host.py` excludes only `pythonw.exe`, `scribe-app.exe` and `scribe-host.exe`), so a native crash may queue a WER report with a minidump under the user's profile that Windows uploads as its diagnostic consent allows. Residue (i) names this gap for the app's console launch and the app SAYS so at start-up; residues (14)–(16) of the replay tool do not cover it, and the tool said nothing. Pattern siblings: `scripts/measure-speakers.py` over an EXPORTED real recording (the labelling path, `kept-recordings.md`) — the same exposure; `run-validation.py` / `build-validation-set.py` take invented or mock content only (pilot plan Constraint 8). Triage: Fix-now (localized, the app's own D10 precedent: name it and say so). /fix decision: Applied — `replay_kept.CRASH_REPORT_NOTE`, printed once just before `_replay_all` (after every refusal and the listing, so before the first recording is read): "[note] crash reports are not excluded for this run (python.exe): if the speech models crash, Windows may keep a crash report holding part of a recording, and send it to Microsoft if its diagnostic settings allow"; the module docstring's custody paragraph names what is NOT enforced; threat model residue (18) (the sibling named there too); flow 27's "no network" non-flow; `docs/testing/kept-recordings.md` (the report's lines, and a Custody bullet naming Settings → Privacy & security → Diagnostics & feedback). Test: `test_a_kept_recording_is_replayed_into_a_numbers_only_row` pins the note once and before the first `[run ]` line (its words avoid every content word `_no_content` checks). NOT done, for the practitioner to decide later if wanted (no MUST-PAUSE — the residue is named and announced): a per-process mitigation (e.g. `WerSetFlags(WER_FAULT_REPORTING_FLAG_NOHEAP)`, or an unhandled-exception filter that terminates before WER) or a `python.exe` exclusion (the `pythonw.exe` breadth residue again, wider). [SUPERSEDED by round 45 PR-HIGH-002, practitioner decision 2026-10-08: the `python.exe` exclusion was chosen, enforced by a refusal in both tools; `CRASH_REPORT_NOTE` removed.]
- **SEC-002** (LOW, availability only, fails closed; `past_sessions.py` `_read_ledger` / `_write_ledger` / `_reset_ledger`): a start-up reset whose `exports-key.dpapi` unlink fails (a scanner's lock) after `exports.enc` went leaves a key with no ledger; when that key was the damage (it does not unwrap), `_read_ledger` sees no ledger and returns no rows (no reset, no line), and every later Export reaches `_write_ledger`, fails the unwrap and refuses as `export_unrecorded` — for good, with nothing telling the practitioner why. Triage: Fix-now. /fix decision: Applied — `_write_ledger` replaces a key that does not unwrap NON-transiently when the ledger is CONFIRMED absent (`_absent`: no row can exist under it); a ledger present, or a transient key read, still raises (the start-up reset stays the only path for a damaged ledger with rows); docstring and the threat model's Export paragraph updated. Tests: `test_a_damaged_ledger_key_with_no_ledger_is_replaced_at_the_next_export` (export succeeds, a fresh key, no row left) and `test_a_damaged_ledger_key_beside_a_ledger_is_never_replaced` (refused, the key and the ledger untouched).

#### Findings

- **PR-MED-001** (slice A01) (MED, behavioral, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/exclusions.py:322`): A differently named interpreter is refused even when its image has a valid WER exclusion. The reader checks only the four predefined names, so both tools incorrectly reject a covered interpreter; rerunning registration cannot resolve this. — Evidence: “`for name in WER_EXCLUDED_APPLICATIONS:`”; line 324 queries only that name, while line 593 checks “`values.get(name) == WER_EXCLUDED_VALUE`” for the running interpreter. Recommendation: Fix-now — Query the running image’s exclusion through the injected seam, retaining DWORD validation and fail-closed handling; test an excluded alternate name. Verified: `surface=code+docs`, `rec=Fix-now — exclusions.py:322 reads only WER_EXCLUDED_APPLICATIONS, so check_tool_wer answers wer_not_excluded for e.g. python3.14.exe and its line says to re-run registration, which never adds that name — the refusal is right (fail closed) but its remedy cannot work`. Weighed, per the composer's brief: the peer's shape (read the running image's own value) is DECLINED and recorded — the refusal is kept and given a remedy that works. Reasons: (1) the reader stays confined to the values this app writes, reads back and on `--unregister` removes, so the tools' gate rests only on state the app manages; a hand-added exclusion for another name is outside that custody (nothing re-checks it, removes it or shows it on the Status tab); (2) widening `WindowsLayer.wer_exclusions` to arbitrary names changes a C6 seam with five fakes for no documented launch — every documented command runs `.venv\Scripts\python.exe` (the venv launcher and the base interpreter are both `python.exe`); (3) it matches the app's own D10 rule, where another interpreter name is an uncovered launch whatever the registry holds. /fix decision: Applied — `check_tool_wer` returns NEW `wer_uncovered_name` for a running image name outside `WER_EXCLUDED_APPLICATIONS`, BEFORE the layer is built or the registry read; its line: "crash reports are checked only for the Python names the register script excludes, not <name> - start this with the developer build's .venv\Scripts\python.exe instead" (no registration remedy); the docstring records the decision. Tests: `test_exclusions.py` (`test_a_name_the_register_script_never_excludes_is_refused_unread` × 3 — the layer factory must not be called; `test_the_uncovered_name_line`; the old `python3.14.exe` → `wer_not_excluded` assertion removed); `test_replay_kept.py` (`test_an_interpreter_name_the_register_script_never_excludes_is_refused` — even with that name excluded in the fake registry: exit 2, the lock never taken, nothing loaded or unwrapped); the two tool test modules now pin `sys.executable` to a venv `python.exe` (`speaker_fakes.VENV_PYTHON`) so they no longer depend on how pytest was started. Documents: threat-model residue (18), `docs/testing/kept-recordings.md` (Refusals), `docs/testing/speaker-measurement.md` ("Running it").
- **PR-LOW-002** (slice A02) (LOW, test-harness, `desktop/tests/test_register_native_host.py:128`): The updated test proves the fourth exclusion is written, but does not prove it is read back. Omitting only `python.exe` from the verification loop would leave these tests passing. — Evidence: line 134 asserts “`registry.keys[_KEY]["python.exe"] == (1, FakeWinreg.REG_DWORD)`”; lines 141–144 inject individual failures only for `scribe-host.exe`, `pythonw.exe`, and `scribe-app.exe`, plus a whole-key failure. Recommendation: Fix-now — Add a dropped or wrong-type `python.exe` case and assert registration reports failure rather than `verified : OK`. Verified: `surface=test-harness`, `rec=Fix-now — test_register_native_host.py:141–144 injects read-back failures only for scribe-host.exe, pythonw.exe, scribe-app.exe and the whole key; nothing proves python.exe is read back (main's `verified` is `not wer_failed`, scripts/register-native-host.py:247)`. /fix decision: Applied — two cases in `test_a_value_that_does_not_read_back_as_dword_1_fails`: `python.exe` dropped after the write, and `python.exe` rewritten as `REG_SZ "1"`, each returning `["python.exe"]` (so `verified : FAIL`). Test only; no smoke step.
- **PR-LOW-003** (slice B01) (LOW, docs-only, `AGENTS.md:58`): The developer launch instructions still incorrectly say `python.exe` is not excluded from Windows Error Reporting, contradicting the round-45 fix and run step 5. — Evidence: “this launch runs `python.exe`, which is not excluded from Windows Error Reporting, and the Status tab says so”; `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/exclusions.py:89–92` now includes `"python.exe"` in the developer exclusions, and lines 541–543 warn about an uncovered launch only when its name is absent from that list. Recommendation: Fix-now — State that the documented console launch is covered once the registration script has been re-run; other interpreter names remain uncovered. Verified: `surface=docs`, `rec=Fix-now — AGENTS.md "Launching the desktop app" still said python.exe "is not excluded … and the Status tab says so", contradicting run step 5 and exclusions.py:89–92`. /fix decision: Applied — the sentence now says the console launch's `python.exe` is excluded once run step 5's register script has been re-run (until then the Status tab warns that crash reports are not excluded), and another interpreter name (`python3.14.exe`, `py.exe`) stays uncovered with the Status tab saying so. Sibling grep (`python.exe … not excluded` across the repository's documents): no other site.

#### Findings

- **SIMP-001** (LOW, `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/exclusions.py` `check_wer` and `check_tool_wer`): round 45's `check_tool_wer` re-spelled `check_wer`'s per-hive read loop verbatim (`found` / `unreadable`, each hive read on its own, only `OSError` caught) — a later change to how a hive is read (round 14 LOW-003's rule) made in one would leave the refusing check and the warning disagreeing. Triage: Fix-now. Simpler shape: one private `_read_wer_hives(layer) -> (found, unreadable)` behind both; `WerReader` moved above it (it now types both callers; `WindowsLayer` satisfies it structurally). Behaviour-preserving: the same hives in the same order, the same caught class (anything else still propagates from `check_wer` and is still caught by `check_tool_wer`'s outer handler), the same results. Pattern siblings (search `wer_exclusions(` in `src`): these two; `register-native-host.py`'s read-back reads values directly through `winreg`, not a layer — not a sibling. /fix decision: Applied. Verification: the FULL desktop suite (`test_exclusions.py`'s `TestCheckWer`, `TestCheckWerProduction` and `TestCheckToolWer` pin both checks, including the per-hive and unreadable cases).

Consciously left alone (each with its reason): the two tools' identical three-line refusal block and their identical `_windows_layer` seams (folding them into one helper would move the seam each tool's tests patch, and two short call sites read clearly — the refusal's behaviour lives once, in `exclusions`); `check_tool_wer` and `tool_wer_refusal_line` as two functions (the code is pinned apart from its wording, as `check_wer` / `ExclusionWarning` are); `_TOOL_WER_LINES` beside `_UNCOVERED_LAUNCH` (different audiences and remedies — the app's start-up line vs a tool's refusal); `_write_ledger`'s replace-when-absent branch (round 44 SEC-002 — the `crypto is None` merge of "no key" and "replaceable key" is already the shortest shape); `speaker_fakes.interpreter_image()` beside the pinned `VENV_PYTHON` (the refusal tests read the name the tool will see, whichever pin is in force); the register script's docstrings (accurate, no code to fold).

SIMPLIFY-ROUND-48 RESULT: 1 finding (CRIT 0 / HIGH 0 / MED 0 / LOW 1) — Fix-now, applied; nothing for a plan task.

## Tasks

Grouped into phases for `/execute-loop` (a review loop and a codex peer pass at each boundary). Shared helpers accumulated in `Key Findings`: the counted-site test in `test_shadow_exits.py` (Phases 1–3), the archive's `read_recording` (Phases 2–4), the public `transcript_wer` (Phase 4), the authored-line pattern of `ui/past_sessions_view.py` (Phase 3). Paths under `desktop/src/scribe_desktop/` unless they start otherwise.
